  - 94,962 combo variants; no two share a piece set, and none has one piece. 810 produce "Win the game"; 709 of those produce nothing containing "infinite", 146 of them two-card. Thassa's Oracle + Demonic Consultation (`742-1295`) produces `["Exile your library", "Win the game"]`. The Tymna cEDH benchmark deck (`tests/fixtures/benchmark/cedh_tymna_thrasios.txt:32-33`) holds both pieces.
  - 205 of the 3,815 two-card variants have at least one superset variant (the same two cards plus more); the most for one pair is 237.
  - Live oracle text, checked against each pattern: Birds of Paradise and Arcane Signet ("Add one mana of any color…"), Nature's Lore ("a Forest card"), Farseek, Winter Orb ("players can't untap more than one land"), Static Orb, Cyclonic Rift, Toxic Deluge, Blasphemous Act ("deals 13 damage to each creature"), Rishkar's Expertise ("Draw cards equal to"), Sylvan Library ("draw two additional cards"), Glorious Anthem, Clone, Entomb. Fire // Ice has `cmc` 4.0.
  - 12 cards match the mass-land-denial `return|destroy|exile (all|each) … land` pattern and mention a graveyard. All but Decree of Annihilation and Fall of the Thran are recursion or graveyard hate, for example Splendid Reclamation, Planar Birth, Titania, World Shaper, Lumra and Haunting Echoes.
  - 428 multi-face cards carry `power` only on their faces.
  - 4 non-land cards are refused as tutors because their search span says "nonland": Night Dealings, Grim Reminder, Selective Memory and Ugin, Eye of the Storms.
- **Overlap with the architecture report and B4:** the land rule (`mana_base._is_land`, the classifier's inline `"land" in type_line`) and the bare-pip colour count are research F1 / §6. B4's patch "multi-face cards are invisible to every synergy detector" points at this package's `_match_text` as the fix. Neither is repeated here; merge them in step 4.
- **Plan note for step 4:** the package cites AD-2, AD-3, AD-7–AD-13 from the deck-assess spine `architecture-Artificial-Planeswalker-2026-07-11/architecture-Artificial-Planeswalker-2026-07-11.md`. There AD-2 is "pure deterministic core, impure edge" and AD-3 is "FormatProfile is passive frozen data", not the companion spine's AD-2/AD-3. That spine is not in this file's `context:` list.
- **Calibration note:** every classifier-vocabulary patch below (mass land denial, Winter Orb, ramp, land fetch, interaction, draw, face power, the two tutor fixes) moves scores. The pattern lists are documented as "provisional v1, tuned by the benchmark pass", so each one re-runs `tests/integration/logic/test_assessment_benchmark.py` and bumps `format_profile_version` per the AD-3 rule if an anchor moves. Batching them into one story means one recalibration.
- **What holds:**
  - **Determinism.** Every output collection is sorted (`match_combos` by `spellbook_id`, name tuples, `WUBRG`-ordered pip signals). `_to_score` rounds half-up, so the result never depends on banker's rounding. Combo credits are multiples of 0.5, so summation order cannot change the result. No set iteration reaches an output.
  - **Purity and layering.** Imports are the standard library, `src.data.schemas` and sibling modules only. There is no I/O, clock or randomness, and inputs are never mutated (`model_copy` for buckets).
  - **Combo credit rules.** Only `included` raises the floor. `almost_included` earns half credit, and `bucket=None` earns nothing. The commander gate credits the command zone once.
  - **No duplication with the validator or synergy.** The package re-implements no legality rule. `classifiers.py` is the only oracle-text vocabulary in it. Its copies of `strip_reminder_text` and `_to_score` are documented and deliberate.
  - **Shipped profiles.** Their weights sum to 1.0 and their cuts are strictly ascending, both pinned by tests.

- [ ] [Review][Decision] Deterministic two-card wins are scored as non-infinite — `combo_type` returns `TWO_CARD_INFINITE` only when a `produces` entry contains "infinite". Thassa's Oracle + Demonic Consultation and 145 other two-card "Win the game" variants become `NON_INFINITE`. They never raise the floor through the early-two-card rule, never set `fast_infinite` (so cEDH candidacy fails for a Thoracle list), never shortcut `speed`, and earn 15 credit instead of 50 plus earliness. It may be part of why the Tymna cEDH benchmark lands in High-Power, a band below Competitive (`profiles.py:147-148`). `combo_type` is internal; no tool output or skill names its tokens. [`src/logic/assessment/combos.py:206-223`; `src/logic/assessment/dimensions.py:394-404`, `:610-618`, `:686`] Options: (a) count a "win the game" entry as equivalent to infinite in `combo_type` and reword the token docstrings to "infinite or game-winning"; (b) add a fourth token (`two_card_win`) and handle it beside `TWO_CARD_INFINITE` at the three sites; (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — a one-line change with no new vocabulary; the benchmark re-run decides whether the profile version moves.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] Two win-route patterns tag most creature decks — `_HAYMAKER_RE` tags every anthem ("Creatures you control get +1/+1": Glorious Anthem, most lords) as `WINCON_FINISHER`. `\ba copy of\b` tags every Clone and token-copy card as `WINCON_COMBO_PIECE`. Almost any creature deck then shows two or three win routes. That silences `wincon_missing`, inflates `resilience` (its route-count term) and raises the consistency win-condition opener term. [`src/logic/assessment/classifiers.py:200-209`] Options: (a) narrow both: the haymaker to a team pump that ends the game (an `until end of turn` pump with trample, or `+X/+X`), and drop the bare `a copy of` pattern, keeping `copy target spell|ability`; (b) keep the anthem tag but require evasion or a size threshold in the same sentence; (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — Craterhoof and Overrun still match, and Kiki-Jiki-style copy combos are covered by Spellbook matching, which already supersedes this tag.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] Spellbook superset variants are each credited — `match_combos` buckets every variant independently. A deck holding a two-card combo also gets `almost_included` credit (15 or 25) for every three-card variant that is the same pair plus one card it lacks, and full credit for each superset it completes. 205 two-card pairs have supersets, up to 237 for one pair. `combo_potential` (0.30 of the Commander weight) then reaches 100 because of how many variants Spellbook lists, not how many combos the deck has, and the `combos` list repeats the same core. [`src/logic/assessment/combos.py:181-198`; `src/logic/assessment/dimensions.py:594-621`] Options: (a) in scoring, skip a matched record whose piece set strictly contains an `included` record's piece set (the output list keeps them); (b) also drop them from the returned `combos`; (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — it fixes the score without changing the combo list the agent shows; (b) changes the tool output and is a separate call.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] The bracket triggers report no cards — `bracket_floor` computes `mass_land_denial_names`, `extra_turn_names` and `early_two_card_infinite_ids`, but `score()` keeps only `floor` and `cedh_candidate`. `CoreAssessment.mass_land_denial` / `extra_turn_chains` are bare booleans, and the tool's flags block (`assess_deck_power.py:589-590`) shows `true` with no card behind it. With the mass-land-denial false positives below, a user told "mass land denial, Bracket 4" cannot see that Splendid Reclamation caused it. The classifier docstrings say the names are "FR23's `flags` explainability". [`src/logic/assessment/scorer.py:137-166`; `src/logic/assessment/dimensions.py:418-428`] Options: (a) carry the three name tuples on `CoreAssessment` and into the flags block (a tool-contract change: skills, `plan_checkpoint`); (b) keep booleans and drop the unused name fields from `BracketFloorSignal`; (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — the names already exist, and an unexplained Bracket-4 verdict is the one an agent most needs to justify.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] `mana_efficiency` is near 0 for most multicolour Commander decks, and the profile comment blames the wrong term — at avgMV 3 with 10 cheap draw/ramp spells the Karsten 99-card regression recommends 38 lands, so a 36-land deck is inside tolerance. The penalty comes from the pip deficits: the provisional Commander anchors (23/30/33, a linear scale of the 60-card values), counted against lands only, with fetchlands and "basic land card" fetches (Fabled Passage, Prismatic Vista) giving zero sources, at 3 points per missing source summed over colours. A three-colour deck loses around 100+ points, while a mono-colour deck keeps 100. `profiles.py` says the zero comes "under the Karsten delta". [`src/logic/assessment/dimensions.py:569-577`; `src/logic/assessment/mana_base.py:266`, `:330-338`; `src/logic/assessment/profiles.py:143-144`] Options: (a) correct the comment, and count a fetchland as a source of each basic type it names (a direct fix; "basic land card" fetches count for every colour); (b) as (a), and replace the provisional Commander anchors with Karsten's published Commander tables; (c) correct the comment only. Recommended: (a) — the comment misleads the next calibration and the fetchland miss is a plain defect; (b) waits for a benchmark case, since the dimension weighs 0.05 in Commander.
- [ ] [Review][Patch] Mass land denial fires on graveyard land recursion — the `return (all|each) … lands?` branch matches "Return all land cards from your graveyard to the battlefield" (Splendid Reclamation, Planar Birth, Titania, World Shaper, Lumra), and the `exile all` branch matches graveyard hate (Haunting Echoes). The floor jumps to 4 and `flags` reports MLD. Require the return branch to send lands to owners' hands, and exclude graveyard-zone wording; keep Armageddon, Sunder, Decree of Annihilation and Fall of the Thran matching. Add the recursion cards as negative cases [`src/logic/assessment/classifiers.py:243`]

- `false` — `aggregate_score` does not check that weights sum to 1.0 (two lenses): profiles are module constants, their sum is pinned by the profile tests, and no caller supplies a profile.
- `false` — the command-zone `have += 1` double-counts a commander already in `deck_cards`: the extra copy can only cover a second required copy of a commander, which no variant has.
- `false` — commander pieces outside `deck_cards` lose their `cmc`, so the earliest turn falls: the edge resolves commanders from mainboard rows, so they are always in `deck_cards`.
- `false` — a one-card infinite is labelled `multi_card_infinite`: the snapshot holds no one-card variant.
- `false` — an empty deck can produce `included` commander combos: variants are fetched by mainboard names, so an empty deck gets none.
- `false` — an inverted `win_turn_band` passes when the deck has no spells: the band is a pinned module constant.
- `false` — a `tier_thresholds` tuple of the wrong length raises `IndexError`: the field is typed `tuple[int, int, int, int]`.
- `low` — `__all__` omits constants that tests import from submodules: they are importable where they live, and a narrower package surface is a choice, not a slip.
- `low` — `aggregate._to_score` restates `dimensions._to_score`, and `_source_text` restates the face join: both copies are documented, and hoisting them adds a shared module for no defect.
- `low` — `score()` re-runs `classify_deck` and `compute_curve` about five times: documented, about 100 cards, and passing the counts down widens five signatures.
- `not a defect` — the package cites a different AD-n namespace than this plan's context (intent-alignment): recorded as the plan note above.
- `not a defect` — the diff leaves out the tests, the edge tool and the B4 modules (intent-alignment): the tests, `assess_deck_power.py`, the benchmark fixtures and `cards.db` were read during triage.

### 2026-11-08 — B7 MCP server core

**Code review complete.** 3 `decision-needed`, 12 `patch`, 0 `defer`, 9 rejected.

- **Group:** B7. **Reviewed revision:** `ee0263d652a46f021515860bbc2a19e3dac60eed` (no change to these files up to `b3b97cb`).
- **Files (2,009 lines):** `src/mcp_server/__init__.py`, `server.py`, `__main__.py`; `src/mcp_server/tools/messages.py`, `initialize_database.py`, `build_search_index.py`, `view_deck.py`.
- **Mode:** no-code, thorough; lenses blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, verification-gap, intent-alignment (none failed). Triage: leave as action items.
- **Overlap, not repeated here (merge in step 4):**
  - The `prune=true` advice the MCP tool cannot follow (three lenses) is research F6 and B6's orphan-vector decision. New evidence for that item: `tests/integration/mcp_server/test_first_run_data_init.py:227` asserts `"prune=true" in updated.message`, so the test pins the unfollowable advice. It changes with whichever B6 option wins.
  - `initialize_database` awaited on the loop despite the `server.py:18-19` docstring is research F6 / §8.
  - A zero-card first import answering `ok` is B3's patch at `scryfall.py:432-490`.
  - `docs/architecture.md` still planning "stdio → HTTP/SSE later" is research F9; the code side is the transport decision below.
  - The tool-docstring status drift below overlaps B8/B9's status-vocabulary focus. It is recorded here because the docstrings live in `server.py`.
- **What holds:**
  - **No per-session state.** `build_server`'s closures capture only the session factory, the connection factory and the embedder test seam. Every deck, format and games value is a call parameter.
  - **stdout is JSON-RPC only.** Every diagnostic in `__main__.py` prints to stderr with `flush=True`, and diagnostics failures are swallowed. The MCP path configures no logging and imports nothing under `src/companion/app/`. The one companion-app import is function-local in `_run_companion` (AD-3).
  - **The emit discipline.** Every mutation wrapper emits `deck_changed` after its session block exits, only on a result that proves a write, as a bounded await (no detached task). The helpers convert failures to statuses.
  - **The viewer is frozen (AD-15).** `view_deck` and its tool carry deprecation banners, and the file write and browser open run in `to_thread`.
  - **Off-loop search work.** The three sqlite-vec tools take their connection on the worker thread.
  - **`server.py`'s 1,221 lines.** Most of it is the LLM-facing tool docstrings, which have to sit at registration. Each wrapper is a 3-10-line forward. Splitting the file would move text without removing any, so no size finding.

- [ ] [Review][Decision] The two setup tools are not serialised — the MCP SDK (1.28) runs each request in its own task (`lowlevel/server.py` `tg.start_soon`), and `test_a_tool_call_is_answered_while_the_index_builds` relies on it. A client retry after a timeout, or the agent firing both, can start a second ~500 MB first-run import. If one fails, its `_clear_cards` (`DELETE FROM cards`) runs while the other is mid-batch. A `rebuild=True` build can also `drop_card_vec_table` while another worker thread embeds on its own connection. [`src/mcp_server/tools/initialize_database.py:182-192`; `src/mcp_server/tools/build_search_index.py:85-94`; `src/mcp_server/server.py:1192`, `:1217`] Options: (a) one process-level lock per tool that queues the second call, which then sees `already_initialized` or an incremental no-op; (b) a lock that answers a new `busy` status (a tool-contract change: skills, docstrings); (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — no new vocabulary, and the queued call's answer is already correct.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] `MCP_TRANSPORT` still selects unsupported HTTP transports, unchecked — `cast(_Transport, os.getenv(...))` passes any string to `FastMCP.run`, so a typo crashes startup with a framework `ValueError` traceback. The module docstring says the env var exists "so HTTP/SSE can swap in later (D7)", against `AGENTS.md`'s stdio-only rule. `SECURITY.md:36-38` documents `sse` / `streamable-http` as honoured "for development" but unauthenticated and unsupported. [`src/mcp_server/__main__.py:3-5`, `:42`, `:122`; `SECURITY.md:36-38`; `.env.example:29`] Options: (a) stdio only: drop `_Transport` and the env read, delete the `SECURITY.md` sentence and the `.env.example` line (the plugin and README `MCP_TRANSPORT=stdio` entries become inert, so drop them too); (b) keep the dev modes, validate the value (unknown → stderr warning, fall back to stdio) and reword the docstring to "development only, unsupported"; (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — one transport is the documented contract, and it removes the only unauthenticated listener the code can still open.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] Setup work sits outside the "never raise" guards — `build_search_index` calls `get_connection()` and `is_database_initialized(conn)` before its `try`; `semantic_search_cards` and `find_similar_cards` call `get_connection()` (and, for the first, `get_embedder()`) in `_run` before the helper; `view_deck` calls `is_database_initialized` outside its `DatabaseError` guard. A sqlite-vec load failure, a locked or unreadable file, or an offline first model load reaches the client as an MCP `isError` text instead of a status. The two semantic result models have no `error` token. [`src/mcp_server/tools/build_search_index.py:76-81`; `src/mcp_server/server.py:1075-1076`, `:1144`; `src/mcp_server/tools/view_deck.py:77-80`; `src/mcp_server/tools/semantic_search.py:79`] Options: (a) move the guards inside the existing `try` in `build_search_index` and `view_deck` (both already have `error`), and add `error` to `SemanticSearchResult` / `SimilarCardsResult`, catching connection and embedder failures there (a tool-contract change: skills, docstrings); (b) fix `build_search_index` and `view_deck` only, and state in the two semantic docstrings that infrastructure failures surface as MCP errors; (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — "never raise" is the stated contract of every tool (`server.py:31-32`), and the semantic tools are the ones most likely to hit a missing extension or model.
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `semantic_search_cards` loads the embedder before the database and index guards — `_run` calls `get_embedder()` before `_semantic_search_helper`, and `Embedder()` loads (on first use, downloads) the ~80 MB model. On a fresh install a first semantic query downloads the model and only then answers `database_not_initialized` / `index_unavailable`; offline it raises instead. The helper's own comment says "Guard the unbuilt/empty index BEFORE embedding". Resolve the embedder only after both guards pass (for example, hand the helper a provider it calls after the guards); a test with an embedder seam that raises should still get `index_unavailable` on an unbuilt index [`src/mcp_server/server.py:1074-1088`, `src/mcp_server/tools/semantic_search.py:201-218`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `initialize_database(update=true)` changes decks without telling the companion — the reconcile repoints and merges `deck_cards` onto surviving canonical rows and deletes the stale cards (B3), but the wrapper never emits `deck_changed`. A companion showing an affected deck keeps the deleted card ids until a reconnect. Emit `deck_changed` with `deck_id=None` ("refetch whatever is active", `contracts.py:897-904`) after an `updated` result, outside the helper's session [`src/mcp_server/server.py:1192`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `build_search_index(rebuild=True)` is never driven — no test passes `rebuild=True` to the tool or the helper. `test_rebuild_clears_hashes_and_full_reembeds` re-types the drop/create/clear sequence against `src.search`. Dropping the forward, the `if rebuild:` branch, or the embedder-before-drop ordering passes the suite. Add a build-then-rebuild test (`cards_indexed == N`, `cards_skipped == 0`), a rebuild whose embedder resolution raises (status `error`, `index_is_populated` still true), and a `{"rebuild": true}` call in `test_build_search_index_round_trip` [`src/mcp_server/tools/build_search_index.py:85-94`, `src/mcp_server/server.py:1217-1219`, `tests/integration/mcp_server/test_first_run_data_init.py`, `tests/integration/test_mcp_tools.py`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `update_deck`'s `deck_id.strip()` emit is untested — both update emit tests send unpadded ids, and the reload-fails test checks only the count. Dropping `.strip()` sends the companion a padded id that matches no active deck. Call `update_deck` with `f"  {deck_id} "` on the normal and reload-fails paths, and assert the recorded emit equals the stripped id [`src/mcp_server/server.py:386-389`, `tests/integration/mcp_server/test_deck_changed_wiring.py`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The setup tools' descriptions misstate what they change — the `initialize_database` tool docstring says an update runs "without dropping anything", and the helper says the upsert "never drops existing rows", but the reconcile deletes stale rows and repoints deck references (the module docstring says so). The comment justifying both `destructiveHint`s says they "wipe-and-rebuild local state", but `initialize_database` upserts and `build_search_index` is incremental unless `rebuild` [`src/mcp_server/server.py:1160-1161`, `:1175-1178`; `src/mcp_server/tools/initialize_database.py:141-143`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] Tool docstrings omit statuses their results return — `clone_deck` has no Args or Returns section at all. The Returns of `lookup_card_by_name`, `search_cards`, `list_decks`, `create_deck`, `clone_deck`, `load_deck`, `update_deck`, `delete_deck`, the three card-mutation tools, `import_decklist`, `analyze_mana_curve`, `detect_synergies` and `validate_deck` omit `database_not_initialized`, which every one of their result `Literal`s carries. `list_decks`, `create_deck`, `load_deck` and the three card-mutation tools also omit `error`. Generate or check the lists against the `Literal`s, not by hand [`src/mcp_server/server.py:216-217`, `:260-263`, `:293-294`, `:318-319`, `:330-337`, `:355-356`, `:379-382`, `:402-403`, `:438-440`, `:486-489`, `:525-526`, `:557-559`, `:883-885`, `:902-904`, `:934-936`; result models in `src/mcp_server/tools/*.py`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] Observational tools carry no `readOnlyHint` — under the MCP defaults (`readOnlyHint=false`, `destructiveHint=true`), clients treat the searches, lookups, `load_deck`, `list_decks`, the analysis and assessment tools, `companion_status` and `view_deck` as possibly destructive and may gate them behind confirmation. Annotate the read-only tools [`src/mcp_server/server.py:199-1158`]

- `false` — `delete_deck`, `remove_card_from_deck` and `set_card_quantity` lack `destructiveHint`: the MCP default for a tool without `readOnlyHint` is `destructiveHint=true`.

- `low` — the diagnostics do not report the semantic index: a new feature, not a defect.
- `low` — a failing `_clear_cards` replaces the import error: Python chains the original, `logger.exception` logs both, and the path needs a second failure on top of the first.
- `low` — the tier-list and groups docstring summaries wrap onto a second line: FastMCP sends the whole docstring, and no client was shown to truncate it.

### 2026-11-08 — B8 MCP deck and companion tools

**Code review complete.** 3 `decision-needed`, 16 `patch`, 0 `defer`, 13 rejected.

- **Group:** B8. **Reviewed revision:** `ee0263d652a46f021515860bbc2a19e3dac60eed` (no change to these files up to `78c9bf1`).
- **Files (2,347 lines):** `src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py` (941), `deck_import.py` (567), `companion.py` (839).
- **Mode:** no-code, thorough; lenses blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, verification-gap, intent-alignment (none failed). Triage: leave as action items.
- **Overlap, not repeated here (merge in step 4):**
  - MCP `list_decks` hydrating every card is B2's `list_deck_summaries` patch.
  - The unbounded `find_by_name_partial` behind `resolve_card` is B2's decision. The capped-count message below is a separate defect at the B8 call sites. B2's option (b) would fix both.
  - `is_database_initialized` outside each helper's `try` is B1's readiness-probe decision. New evidence for it: `set_active_deck`'s docstring promises "Never raises. A `DatabaseError` becomes `error`", but its probe at `companion.py:170` runs unguarded. The other pre-reads in the card tools are the separate patch below.
  - The tool descriptions the LLM reads are the `server.py` wrapper docstrings, which B7's status-list patch covers. The result-model docstrings below are the B8 half: they reach the client as output-schema descriptions.
  - Bare `4 Lightning Bolt` lines (no set or collector) are rejected by `_CARD_LINE_RE`. That is the documented format contract, and it is still listed as open in the Arena import/export research, so it is a feature, not a finding.
- **What holds:**
  - **AD-3 and statelessness.** `companion.py` imports only the leaf (`src.companion.client`, `contracts`). No module keeps per-call state; the module-level tables are constant message sentences.
  - **The emit discipline.** No helper emits. The wrappers emit after their session block, on a result that proves a write (B7). `update_deck`'s committed-but-reload-failed path answers `ok` with `deck=None` on purpose, so the emit still fires. `import_decklist` emits on `imported_lines > 0`, which correctly includes `partial`.
  - **Push payload bounds.** The push helpers trust the contract models, whose caps FastMCP applies at the tool boundary (`contracts.py`, B10). Every push result reports counts, never contents (CM-1). `set_active_deck` bounds every echoed id and name to 48 characters and closes its session before the HTTP call.
  - **One transaction per import.** Lines resolve first, against an in-memory `occupied` set seeded from the deck. Then one bulk write runs, and a failed write turns every pending line into `error`, with nothing added.
  - **Size.** No file is oversized for its job. About half of `companion.py` is wire-visible docstrings, and the four push tools already share `_execute_push` and `_push_messages`.

- [ ] [Review][Decision] Two printings of one card in one Arena export import only the first line — card rows are oracle identities, so `2 Island (ELD) 250` and `2 Island (THB) 251` resolve to the same `card_id`. The second line answers `exists` ("use set_card_quantity"), and the deck comes back short with status `partial`. Arena lists each printing separately, so a deck with mixed-art basics hits this on an ordinary export. The docstring states the rule ("A card named twice for the same board within one export is `exists` on its second line"). [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_import.py:354-356`, `:480-486`] Options: (a) sum the quantities of lines that resolve to the same `(card_id, sideboard)` within one export, check the sum against `MAX_CARD_QUANTITY`, and keep `exists` only for rows already in the deck (a tool behaviour change: docstrings, skills); (b) keep the rule, but say in the line message that this is a second printing and that its copies were not added; (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — the export is one deck, and the agent cannot tell from `exists` that copies are missing.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] `set_card_quantity` answers `card_not_found` for a known card that is not in the requested board — `remove_card_from_deck` answers `not_in_deck` for the same situation, and `DeckCardResult` already carries that token. An agent reading `card_not_found` may retry with another name, when the fix is `add_card_to_deck` or the other board. This is the spec's wording; the quick-wins work flagged the difference and got no ruling. [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:718-720`, `:795-801`, `:825-833`] Options: (a) answer `not_in_deck` there too (a tool-contract change: `server.py` docstring, skills, tests); (b) keep `card_not_found` and leave the docstring note as the record. Recommended: (a) — one fact, one token, and the message already says "not in the {board}".
- [ ] [Review][Decision] The deck tools spell the same fact with different tokens — a missing deck is `not_found` on `DeckResult` / `DeckDeleteResult` but `deck_not_found` on the card, import and companion results. A missing card is `card_not_found` on `DeckCardResult` but `not_found` on an import line. Each tool is internally consistent, and the messages say what is missing. [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:91`, `:105`, `:138-139`; `src/mcp_server/tools/deck_import.py:31`, `:311-312`; `src/mcp_server/tools/companion.py:115`] Options: (a) unify on `deck_not_found` / `card_not_found` everywhere (a tool-contract change across five tools, their docstrings, the skills and the tests); (b) leave the tokens and list them side by side in the magic-deckbuilding skill; (c) leave it. Recommended: (c) — renaming tokens the agent already handles buys little, and the messages disambiguate; revisit if the `set_card_quantity` decision above opens a tool-contract story anyway.
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `load_deck`, `delete_deck` and `set_active_deck` do not strip `deck_id` — `update_deck`, `clone_deck`, the three card tools and `import_decklist` do. So `" <uuid> "` works for add and update but answers `not_found` / `deck_not_found` here, and `set_active_deck` echoes the padded id. Strip at entry in all three, and grep every helper for the pattern [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:399`, `:550`; `src/mcp_server/tools/companion.py:149`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The card tools' pre-reads sit outside any `DatabaseError` guard — `get_deck` / `get_deck_with_cards` and `resolve_card` run unguarded in `add_card_to_deck`, `set_card_quantity` and `remove_card_from_deck`, so a lock or I/O error reaches the client as an MCP `isError` instead of `status="error"`. `load_deck`, `update_deck`, `clone_deck` and `import_decklist` guard their reads. Fix together with B1's probe decision [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:649`, `:657`, `:766`, `:774`, `:890`, `:898`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `create_deck` stores `format` as sent — no case folding and no length cap, while `list_decks(format=…)` filters with an exact `DeckModel.format == format_filter`. A deck created as `"Commander"` never appears under `list_decks(format="commander")`, which answers `empty`; `validate_deck` and the assessment normalise it, so nothing else notices. Normalise (strip, casefold) and cap `format` on create, and fold the filter the same way [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:383`, `:294`; `src/data/repositories/deck.py:364-365`, `:411-412`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `set_active_deck` releasing its session before the HTTP call is never checked — `_ClientStub` records only the request. Deleting `await session.close()` (or moving it after the call) passes the suite, and the read transaction then stays open through the companion round trip (up to 10 s). Record `session.in_transaction()` inside the stubbed client and assert it is `False` [`src/mcp_server/tools/companion.py:202-205`, `tests/integration/mcp_server/test_companion_tool.py:229-367`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The import's per-line `quantity < 1` and `> 250` branches are never driven — the only quantity test sends 5,000 digits, which the length guard stops first. Dropping the `< 1` branch makes a `0 …` line raise a `ValidationError` from `DeckCardEntry` and fail the whole import. Dropping the `> 250` branch stores 251 copies. Add a test parametrised over `0` and `251` that expects an `invalid` line, the next line imported, and the deck otherwise unchanged [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_import.py:247-274`, `tests/integration/mcp_server/test_deck_import_tool.py`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The `DatabaseError` write arms of `add_card_to_deck` and `remove_card_from_deck` are untested — the existing parametrisation plants write failures only through `set_card_quantity` and `update_deck`. Deleting either `except DatabaseError` arm passes the suite. Add both tools to `test_a_database_error_in_the_new_tools_is_silent_and_leaves_the_deck_alone` (`status == "error"`, no emit, deck unchanged) [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:683-689`, `:921-927`; `tests/integration/mcp_server/test_deck_changed_wiring.py:689-719`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `add_card_to_deck` reports every `IntegrityError` as `exists` — foreign keys are enforced, so a deck deleted between the `get_deck` pre-check and the insert also raises `IntegrityError`. The agent is then told the card "is already in the mainboard; use set_card_quantity". Re-check the deck in that branch and answer `deck_not_found` when it is gone [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:674-682`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `clone_deck` without a name can exceed the 100-character name cap — the repository default is `f"{source.name} (copy)"`, so a 94-100 character source gives up to 107 characters, and cloning a clone stacks `" (copy) (copy)"`. Bound the default name to `MAX_DECK_NAME_CHARS` [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:524-539`, `src/data/repositories/deck.py:162`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The `ambiguous` message reports the capped count — `resolve_card` returns `matches[:_MAX_MATCHES]`, and every caller formats `len(matches)`, so a name hitting 300 cards says "matches 10 cards". Say "more than 10" when the list was truncated (B2's partial-match decision (b) gives the same wording) [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:270`, `:664`, `:781`, `:905`; `src/mcp_server/tools/deck_import.py:472`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `remove_card_from_deck` keeps its own copies of the shared wording — it builds the `ambiguous` and `card_not_found` messages and the board name inline instead of calling `ambiguous_message`, `card_not_found_message` and `_location`, which exist so the deck tools word these answers the same way. The next wording change will miss this copy [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:899-918`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `create_deck` and `update_deck` normalise metadata differently — `update_deck` treats a blank `strategy` as clear, but `create_deck` stores `"   "`. Neither strips tags, drops blank ones or removes duplicates, so `["", " ", "aggro", "aggro"]` is stored as sent. Use one normaliser for both paths [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:374-388`, `:473-474`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `add_card_to_deck(commander=True, sideboard=True)` is accepted — the assessment treats a sideboard commander flag as no commander (`assess_deck_power.py:514-521`, "sideboard rows are never commanders"), and the import never sets it. Answer `invalid` for the combination [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:597-635`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The 250-line pre-count includes `About` metadata lines — it counts every non-blank, non-header line, but the parser skips `Name …` lines under `About`, so a valid export near 250 card lines is rejected for "more than 250 card lines". Count with the parser's own rules [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_import.py:383-395`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `_line_result` hides unexpected statuses — the `elif` chain maps each token to itself and ends in `elif == "error"` then `else: "error"`, so a status that should never reach it (`deck_not_found`, `unchanged`, `not_in_deck`) silently becomes `error`. The loop also builds a throwaway `DeckCardResult` only to project it back. Build the line results directly, or use a mapping that fails loudly [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_import.py:309-340`, `:467-507`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `_ECHO_LIMIT` and `SetActiveDeckResult.deck_name` justify the bound with a stale fact — both say `create_deck` "never caps" the name length, but `MAX_DECK_NAME_CHARS = 100` applies on create and update. Long names now come only from older rows and the `clone_deck` default (patch above). Correct the rationale [`src/mcp_server/tools/companion.py:62-76`, `:103-105`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] Result-model docstrings misstate their statuses and callers — these reach the client as output-schema descriptions. `DeckResult` names only `create_deck` / `load_deck` / `update_deck` (`clone_deck` returns it too), and its status list omits `error` and `database_not_initialized`. `DeckListResult` and `DeckDeleteResult` omit the same two tokens. The `clone_deck` helper has a one-line docstring with no Args or Returns and no word about the `" (copy)"` default. `_execute_push` says the event class, kind and payload type "arrive here as arguments", but only the built event does [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:62-107`, `:524-525`; `src/mcp_server/tools/companion.py:518-523`]

**Rejected**

- `overlap` — MCP `list_decks` loads every card of every deck: already B2's patch.
- `overlap` — `find_by_name_partial` is unbounded under `resolve_card`: already B2's decision.
- `overlap` — `set_active_deck`'s readiness probe sits outside its `try`: B1's probe decision (the new evidence is in the overlap list above).
- `not a defect` — bare-name Arena lines are rejected: the documented line format; still open as a feature in the Arena research.
- `low` — a `DatabaseError` resolving one import line moves on to the next: per-line `error` is the designed contract (`_error_line`), and the bulk write stays all-or-nothing.
- `low` — `set_card_quantity`'s "(was N)" can be stale if another writer changes the row mid-call: message text only, under a concurrent write to the same row.
- `low` — a Commander-section card already in the deck never gets its flag: the documented `exists` rule; remove and re-add works, and a fix adds a flag-update path.
- `false` — `launch_command` points at the wrong directory on a non-editable install: the supported shapes are a clone and a plugin `server/` tree, both run through `uv run --directory`. A `$` in a POSIX path is `low` and needs per-shell quoting.
- `low` — `CompanionStatusResult` reserves an `error` it never mints: documented as reserved, and harmless.
- `false` — the push results have no `error` token: AD-8 by design; there is no database to fail.
- `false` — `import_decklist` emits on `partial`, not only `ok`: a `partial` import wrote rows, and the emit keys on a write.
- `not a defect` — the LLM-facing tool descriptions are in `server.py`, outside the group (intent-alignment): B7's status-list patch covers them.
- `not a defect` — the diff leaves out the tests, `server.py` and `contracts.py` (intent-alignment): the wrappers, the repositories, `assess_deck_power.py` and the cited tests were read during triage.

### 2026-11-08 — B9 MCP card and analysis tools

**Code review complete.** 3 `decision-needed`, 18 `patch`, 0 `defer`, 14 rejected.

- **Group:** B9. **Reviewed revision:** `ee0263d652a46f021515860bbc2a19e3dac60eed` (no change under `src/` up to `4f9648c`).
- **Files (2,703 lines):** `src/mcp_server/tools/assess_deck_power.py` (830), `compare_deck_power.py` (430), `deck_analysis.py` (354), `find_similar.py` (460), `semantic_search.py` (268), `card_search.py` (249), `card_lookup.py` (112).
- **Mode:** no-code, thorough; lenses blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, verification-gap, intent-alignment (none failed). Triage: leave as action items.
- **Overlap, not repeated here (merge in step 4):**
  - The raw format key in the JSON path (`"Standard"` matches nothing; `lookup_card_by_name` does not blank-normalise `format`) is B2's `card.py:66` patch and B6's semantic-format patch. The four B9 call sites are `card_lookup.py:80`, `:84`, `card_search.py:179`, `semantic_search.py:187` and `find_similar.py:359`.
  - `validate_deck` checking `game.strip()` but passing the raw list is B4's `games` patch.
  - The unbounded partial-name read is B2's `find_by_name_partial` decision. New evidence: `find_similar._resolve_seed` repeats it in raw SQL (`find_similar.py:253-258`, `fetchall()` with no `LIMIT`), so that decision's fix has to cover both resolvers.
  - The semantic tools leaking failures as MCP errors is B7's "never raise" decision. New evidence: the guard gap is wider than setup. `embedder.encode`, `_resolve_seed`, `get_card_vector` and `hybrid_search` all run unguarded (`semantic_search.py:220-231`, `find_similar.py:387-423`), so a locked database during a build also escapes.
  - The readiness probe outside each helper's `try` is B1's decision. New evidence: in `compare_deck_power`, a `DatabaseError` from side A's probe (`assess_deck_power.py:700`) escapes the whole call, and side B never runs.
  - `exact` with duplicate colours is B2's patch. `colors=[]` with `exact` on the semantic path is B6's patch.
  - `lookup_card_by_name` answering `found` rather than `ok`, and a blank name as `not_found` where its siblings say `invalid`, fall under B8's token decision. The skills document `found` as the one deliberate exception.
- **What holds:**
  - **Observational analysis.** No helper writes. The only session mutation is `session.rollback()` in `assess_deck_power`'s two `DatabaseError` arms, which restores the session that `compare_deck_power` shares. It does not change a deck. The analysis tools pass the mainboard only to the curve and synergy logic.
  - **Layering and statelessness.** The seven modules import only `src.data`, `src.logic`, `src.search` and sibling tool modules. Nothing is retained between calls; `format`, `games` and every filter are parameters.
  - **Deterministic assessment output.** The comparison diffs are sorted, every delta is computed as b − a from the two blocks, and `format_mismatch` stops an implicit cross-format compare.
  - **Bounded sqlite-vec work.** Both sync tools reject `limit` outside 1..50 before any query, and guard `database_not_initialized` and `index_unavailable` first. `find_similar` never embeds; it reads the stored vector.
  - **Size.** No file is oversized for its job. About half of `assess_deck_power.py` is the AD-7 result models and their wire-visible docstrings.

- [ ] [Review][Decision] A failed combo read scores the deck as having no combos, at high confidence — the edge probes availability with `get_snapshot_state`, then calls `get_variants_for_names`, which swallows `OperationalError` and returns `()` (`combo_snapshot.py:151-154`). So a "database is locked" or another `OperationalError` after a good probe gives `combo_potential` 0, no combos in the flags and no `combo_data_unavailable` reason. The module's own docstring says unavailability is "never inferred from an empty `get_variants_for_names` result". [`src/data/repositories/combo_snapshot.py:151-154`; `src/mcp_server/tools/assess_deck_power.py:355-361`, `:775-787`] Options: (a) let the variant read report failure (raise, or return a flag), and have the edge set `combo_data_unavailable` so the deck still scores at lower confidence; (b) let the `OperationalError` propagate to the edge's existing `DatabaseError` arm, which answers `error`; (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — it is AD-6's degradation path, it adds no vocabulary, and the score stays useful.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] `validate_deck` defaults to Standard and ignores the deck's stored format — with `format` omitted it checks against `"standard"` (`deck_analysis.py:276`, `:311`), while `assess_deck_power` resolves the explicit parameter, then the stored `Deck.format`, then the commander flag. A 100-card Commander deck validated without a format comes back with Standard size, copy-limit and legality violations, all true of Standard and irrelevant to the deck. The wrapper and skill tables document the default, and the skills list `format` as required, so an agent following them passes it. [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_analysis.py:276`, `:311`; `src/mcp_server/server.py:910-911`] Options: (a) make `format` optional and fall back to the stored format, then `standard` (a tool-contract change: docstring, skills, tests); (b) keep the default and name the stored format in the message when they differ ("validated as standard; this deck is stored as commander"); (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — the two deck-reading tools should resolve the format the same way, and the stored format is the user's own statement.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] `lookup_card_by_name` does not validate `games` — every other `games` tool answers `invalid` for an unknown platform, and four skills say so ("returns `invalid` from every tool that accepts `games`"). `lookup_card` passes the list straight to the LIKE filter, so `games=["mtga"]` answers "No card found matching 'Sol Ring'". `CardLookupResult` has no `invalid` token. [`src/mcp_server/tools/card_lookup.py:37`, `:80`, `:84`; `.claude/skills/magic-deckbuilding/SKILL.md:198-199`, `format-legality/SKILL.md:347-348`, `mana-curve-analysis/SKILL.md:186-187`, `synergy-discovery/SKILL.md:164-165`] Options: (a) add `invalid` to `CardLookupResult` and reject an unknown platform as the siblings do (a tool-contract change: `server.py` docstring, skills, tests); (b) answer `not_found` with the "Invalid game" message; (c) leave the code and correct the skill sentence. Recommended: (a) — it makes the skills' rule true, and the agent can tell a bad parameter from a missing card.
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The skills say `search_cards.page_size` is "silently clamped" to 50, but the tool rejects it — `_validation_error` answers `invalid` for `page_size > 50` (the comment at `:29-30` says this is on purpose, and the wrapper says "max 50"). All four tool tables and three "notes that bite" paragraphs tell the agent the opposite, so an agent asking for 100 gets an error the skill said would not happen. Correct the table cells and the notes [`.claude/skills/magic-deckbuilding/SKILL.md:185`, `:194-195`; `mana-curve-analysis/SKILL.md:159`, `:182`; `synergy-discovery/SKILL.md:147`, `:158-159`; `format-legality/SKILL.md:308`, `:345`; `src/mcp_server/tools/card_search.py:125-126`]

- `false` — the analysis tools' `DatabaseError` arms need `assess_deck_power`'s rollback: each analysis call owns a session that the wrapper closes, and nothing runs on it afterwards.

- `low` — `compare_deck_power` still assesses side B after side A answers `database_not_initialized`: one extra readiness probe on an empty database, and the answer is right.
- `not a defect` — `compare_deck_power` runs the whole assess pipeline twice (intent-alignment): two decks need two assessments.
- `not a defect` — the diff leaves out `server.py`, the skills and the tests (intent-alignment): the wrappers, the five skills, `combo_snapshot.py` and `card.py` were read during triage, and the verification-gap lens read the seven test suites (its citations were spot-checked).

### 2026-11-08 — B10 Companion contracts and client

  - The 2026-09-03 audit's P2 rider 18 (`live_instance` and `_send` each opening an `AsyncClient`) is closed: every request goes through `_shared_client`. Its one leftover is F7 below.
- **What holds:**
  - **The import boundary (AD-3).** `contracts.py` imports only `typing` and `pydantic`, `discovery.py` adds `src.paths`, and `client.py` adds `httpx` and the two sibling leaves. The research pass confirmed the importer side: `src/mcp_server` imports only the leaf, apart from the AD-14 dispatcher's function-local import, which the boundary test exempts by name.
  - **The AD-9 notifier.** `notify_deck_changed` is a bounded `await` with no detached task. Its 1 s budget is a constant no caller can widen, and every exception is caught and logged. The staleness window ("stale until the next event or a WebSocket reconnect", no UI warning) is stated at both swallow sites and matches the spine word for word. F6 is about which token it logs, not about the window.
  - **AD-8 and AD-4.** The outcome vocabulary is closed at five. The only retry is one, on `403`, and it re-reads discovery on each attempt. The token appears in one header and in no log line. Identity is the echoed `instance_id`, a parse failure means *app not running*, the write is temp-then-`os.replace` in the target directory, and removal is ownership-guarded.
  - **The push payload caps B8 relies on.** Every string and every list on the push wire is capped, and the 64 KB envelope cap is enforced before parsing (`body_cap`). The one unbounded field is `SwapItem`'s quantities (F10). Blank `category` / `note` and an empty `card_id` pass the contract, but the UI handles all three on purpose (`filled()`, `noteOf`, `cards.ts:262`).
  - **Size.** `contracts.py` is about 300 lines of code. The rest is docstrings, most of which ship verbatim as schema descriptions. The module splits cleanly into three blocks: the REST bodies, the envelope and its payloads, and the receipt. A split would move the OpenAPI source for no gain, so none is proposed. The prose problems are F12 to F15.

- [ ] [Review][Decision] On Windows, publishing the discovery file fails while any reader holds it open, and the launch aborts. `write_discovery`'s `os.replace` raises `PermissionError` whenever another process has `companion.json` open, because CPython opens files without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`. Every agent push, every notify, `live_instance` and an AV or indexer scan all open it. The docstring names this failure and lets it abort the launch, on the grounds that a loud failure is the diagnosable one. A relaunch works. [`src/companion/discovery.py:145-148`, `:169`] Options: (a) retry `os.replace` a few times over about 200 ms on `PermissionError`, then raise as today; (b) keep the loud failure and the docstring as they are. Recommended: (a). The reader's hold lasts microseconds, so a short bounded retry removes a spurious launch failure, and a lasting lock still fails loudly.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] `ActiveDeckSetReceipt`'s `extra="forbid"` rests on a false premise. The comment says the client and the backend ship "in the SAME installed package", so "there is no version skew". But a plugin update restarts the MCP server on new code while a companion launched from the old tree keeps running. The sibling `EventIngestReceipt` faces the same skew and stays open. Under `forbid`, a receipt from a skewed backend with an extra key turns a deck switch that was stored and broadcast into `backend_error`. [`src/companion/contracts.py:320-327`; `src/companion/client.py:488-496`] Options: (a) drop `forbid` so the receipt matches `EventIngestReceipt` (an OpenAPI change, so regenerate); (b) keep `forbid` and rewrite the rationale to name the skew it accepts. Recommended: (a). The switch did happen, so reporting failure would be the wrong answer, and the two receipts should treat skew the same way.
- [ ] [Review][Patch] Nothing checks that the published discovery file is mode `0o600`. On POSIX the file mode is the only thing keeping the bearer token from other local users. The write tests check content, atomicity and temp-file litter, and the only `chmod` in `test_discovery.py:133-137` plants a file for the unreadable-read case. Replacing `mkstemp` with a plain `open` and dropping the `chmod` would pass the suite. Add a POSIX-only test (skipped on `nt`, like line 128) that asserts `S_IMODE(st_mode) == 0o600` after `write_discovery`, including the case where a `0o644` file is already at the target [`src/companion/discovery.py:152-169`, `tests/unit/companion/test_discovery.py`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `read_discovery` and `remove_discovery` promise "never raises" but guard path resolution against `OSError` only. `paths.data_dir()` calls `Path.expanduser()`, which raises `RuntimeError` when no home directory can be found (for example, POSIX `PLANESWALKER_DATA_DIR=~nosuchuser/x`). That error would escape through `push_event` and `set_active_deck`, which FR-12 says never break an agent turn. Catch `RuntimeError` beside `OSError` at both guards [`src/companion/discovery.py:199-203`, `:244-248`; `src/paths.py:39-41`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The authenticated push uses the probe's 2 s read deadline under a 10 s whole-call budget. `_send` defaults to `PROBE_TIMEOUT` (`read=2.0`), and the backend replies only after its sequential fan-out (`ws.py:398`). So a fan-out that takes longer than 2 s is reported as `backend_error` for a push that was delivered. `_PUSH_TOTAL_SECONDS`'s "clears both attempts" rationale then never comes into play. Give the authenticated verbs their own read deadline, sized against the 10 s budget [`src/companion/client.py:64-78`, `:286`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The notifier's connect timeout equals its whole-call budget. Both are 1.0 s, and the module says a dead loopback port takes about 2 s to refuse on Windows, which is the ordinary state after a crash. Which fires first decides whether a stale file is logged as `app_not_running` or as `backend_error`. The mutation is unaffected, but the logged token is a coin toss. Give `notify_deck_changed` a connect deadline below `_NOTIFY_TOTAL_SECONDS` [`src/companion/client.py:64`, `:80`, `:457-461`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `reset_shared_client` is documented "test isolation only", but production calls it. `app/server.py:261-263` runs `live_instance` under `asyncio.run`, then forgets the client without closing it. A pooled keep-alive socket to whatever answered the probe is orphaned. Close the client inside the probe's own `asyncio.run` (`aclose_shared_client`), and correct the docstring [`src/companion/client.py:162-165`; `src/companion/app/server.py:259-263`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The two outcome mappers and the two attempt functions are near-copies. `_outcome_for` / `_active_deck_outcome_for` and `_attempt` / `_active_deck_attempt` differ only in the receipt model, the method, the path and a log word. The sibling's docstring justifies the copy with a claim that holds only one way: `EventIngestReceipt` is open, so it parses an `ActiveDeckSetReceipt` body without error. The next status-mapping change will land in one copy, which is the repo's repeated-pattern pitfall. Make one mapper and one attempt, taking the receipt model, method and path [`src/companion/client.py:296-343`, `:469-512`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `HealthResponse.clients` has no lower bound. Both receipts declare `ge=0`, but this field does not, so `companion_status` relays a negative tab count from a buggy responder that echoed the right `instance_id`. Add `ge=0` (an OpenAPI change, so regenerate) [`src/companion/contracts.py:50`; `src/mcp_server/tools/companion.py:808`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `SwapItem.out_qty` / `in_qty` have no upper bound. Python ints are unbounded, and a 300-digit quantity fits well inside the 64 KB envelope, so the swap row renders "Out · 1e+300 copies". Cap both at a deck-plausible ceiling declared in the leaf (the MCP tools use 250). This is an OpenAPI change, so regenerate [`src/companion/contracts.py:690-691`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The `DEFAULT_TITLE_BY_KIND` import-time pin is a bare `assert`. `python -O` strips it, yet the docstring calls it "a stronger guarantee than a test". Raise explicitly, or soften the claim (`test_contracts.py` pins the table anyway) [`src/companion/contracts.py:1299-1312`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] Three published descriptions contradict the code, and these ship in `openapi.json` / `types.d.ts`. First, `ErrorResponse` says `database_unavailable` covers "an unhandled backend fault", but `errors.py` maps unhandled faults to `500 internal_error`, the next bullet. Second, `SuggestionsPayload` says "the view skips an empty push", but `SuggestionsView` renders AD-7's deliberate "came back empty" line, so `displayed` is the right outcome. Third, `ActiveDeckChangedPayload` / `Event` describe a "cleared" `None` case that no producer emits: the route broadcasts a validated non-null id, and `ActiveDeckRequest` has no clearing verb. Say "reserved" for the third. Then regenerate [`src/companion/contracts.py:182-183`, `:801-802`, `:932-933`, `:1207-1208`; `src/companion/app/errors.py:482-543`; `ui/src/containers/SuggestionsView/SuggestionsView.tsx:456`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `ErrorResponse`'s published prose carries tunable, measured numbers: "79 cards in the shipped corpus", twice, and the 30 s / doubling / 300 s backoff. `SessionTicket` keeps its TTL off the wire because "a number there would be a wire commitment". Retuning the backoff, or a Scryfall refresh, leaves the schema description wrong with no test to notice. Move the numbers below `Attributes:` or into a comment, then regenerate [`src/companion/contracts.py:163-164`, `:175-179`, `:352-357`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The wire-edit maintenance notes are incomplete. The "eight edits" list for a new `ErrorReason` counts nine and omits `ui/src/state/cards.ts`'s `CARD_READ_IS_RETRYABLE` (`satisfies Record<ErrorReason, boolean>`), the published `ErrorResponse` bullet and the regeneration. `HealthResponse`, `ActiveDeck`, `ActiveDeckRequest` and `SessionTicket` are route models whose docstrings ship, but they lack the `# WIRE-VISIBLE` marker that every other published class carries [`src/companion/contracts.py:116-122`, `:22`, `:209`, `:245`, `:333`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] Three internal rationale comments state the wrong reason. First, `HealthResponse.clients` says `Optional` lets an older reader parse a newer body. Pydantic ignores extra keys by default, so `Optional` actually serves the newer reader of an older body. Second, `_outcome_for` says the error body is "written for humans", but `ErrorResponse` is a machine token by contract. Third, the module says authenticated sends skip the probe because "a foreign loopback process already runs as the same user". That is false on a multi-user POSIX host. The real reason, that a stale file's token belongs to a dead process, goes unstated [`src/companion/contracts.py:36-39`; `src/companion/client.py:9-13`, `:299-301`]

**Rejected**

- `false` — blank `category` / `note` render an empty badge or line (blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter): `Badge` drops whitespace through `filled()`, and `TierListView.noteOf` folds a blank note to `null` by design.

- `low` — every `read_discovery` creates the data directory: documented and accepted in `discovery_path`.
- `low` — `ts` accepts any year: producers stamp `now(UTC)`, only a token holder can send, and a skew policy adds clock rules.
- `low` — the probe buffers a large foreign body: the 5 s total cap applies on loopback, and a streaming byte cap adds complexity.
- `low` — `set_active_deck` does not compare the echoed `deck_id` with the request: the same-package backend echoes what it stored, and the check adds plumbing for no demonstrated failure.

The file check runs before the first connection, so a fresh install gets no zero-byte `cards.db`.

The PUT captures `slot.deck_id` once, before its broadcast awaits. The UI re-reads `GET /api/active-deck` on every `active_deck_changed`, so broadcasts that arrive out of order cannot leave a tab on the wrong deck.

But every test in `TestThePutBroadcasts` sends one `PUT` against `FakeConnection`s that never yield. Moving the read below the `await`, or answering `slot.deck_id` directly, would pass the suite. - `false` — concurrent `PUT /api/active-deck` broadcasts arriving out of order leave a tab on the wrong deck: the UI reads no `active_deck_changed` payload. It re-drives the boot from `GET /api/active-deck` (`connection.ts:143-145`), so the last refetch reads the slot's final value.
- `false` — `CARD_CACHE_CONTROL`'s hour serves stale legalities after a re-import: the SPA's only fetch helper sends `cache: 'no-store'` (`ui/src/api/client.ts:373-412`), so it never reads a browser-cached card.
- `false` — the SPA fallback 404s a deep link whose last segment has a dot: the SPA has no path routing (no `pushState` or `location.pathname` in `ui/src`), so no client route carries a deck id.
- `false` — `session_factory` after `dispose` builds an engine nobody disposes: uvicorn runs the lifespan shutdown only after in-flight requests finish, and the docstring states the post-dispose behaviour on purpose.
- `false` — the 9.9 s and 9.8 s figures disagree: 9.9 s is the burst's duration and 9.8 s the last tile's start (98 intervals of 0.1 s).
- `low` — the launch line, and `--open`'s tab, come before the lifespan (two lenses). It is real: uvicorn exits `3` after "Application startup failed". But it needs a discovery-write `OSError`, the error follows on stderr at once, and the fix moves the announcement into the lifespan. B10's discovery-retry decision would shrink it further.
- `low` — the existence check runs once, so a `cards.db` deleted mid-run is recreated at zero bytes (two lenses): Windows refuses the delete while the pool holds the file. On POSIX, a zero-byte file reads as not initialized to both shells, and `initialize_database` fills it.
- `low` — a URI-mode `file:…?uri=true` URL fails the existence check for good: no documented setting uses URI mode, and the fix is a new branch.
- `low` — one INFO line per data request while the database is absent: the poller backs off to 30 s, and uvicorn's access log already writes one line per request. A once-only flag adds state.
- `low` — "the lifespan never ran" is handled four ways (`get_session`, `read_card_image`, `_slot` / `_store`, `read_health`): unreachable on every supported path, and a shared helper is new code.
- `low` — `face` has no upper bound, so a huge value logs a few cache-read WARNINGs before `404 no_image_data`: no producer sends one, and a bound changes the OpenAPI contract.
- `not a defect` — the packet carries module source only, without `errors.py`, the tests, `openapi.json` or the prior reviews (intent-alignment): descriptive. All four were read during triage.

  - **B7 / AD-15:** `scripts/view_deck.py` is the CLI beside the frozen viewer.
- **What holds:**
  - **`build_plugin.py`.** It cleans only its managed outputs, so a locked `.venv` cannot abort a half-built tree. It writes LF on every OS, and it aborts on a missing `src/viewer/` or SPA bundle (fired by `TestTheBundleGuardsFire`).
  - **Migrations.** Five of the six `migrate_*` scripts are idempotent per column. The `card_vec` table is idempotent through `IF NOT EXISTS`.
  - **`probe_harness.py`.** It owns its argv. Any exit other than 0 or 1 returns before scoring, and the collected count is cross-checked against the run's own summary.
  - **`vitest_probe_harness.py`.** It owns its cwd, refuses vacuous or contradictory expectations, and is unit-tested with fixture transcripts.
  - **`cdp_harness.py`.** `refetch` and `push --arm drain` are gated against the operator's data dir. `refetch` starts the companion inside its `try`. The drain arm has a positive control and a valid-run floor.

- [ ] [Review][Decision] `--expect-green` has no exit-code veto. `_check` complains only when FAILED or ERROR lines were parsed, so a run that exits 1 without printing either would be certified green, as long as its summary tally matches the collection count. The vitest half closes this hole (`vitest_probe_harness.py:338-353`). No path to an exit 1 with neither line was shown, though: `-rfE` prints one for every failure and error. [`scripts/probe_harness.py:202`] Options: (a) add the veto, so a green expectation with exit 1 is a complaint; (b) also count `failed` and `errors` from the summary line; (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — one clause, the same rule as its sibling, and it needs no proof that the path exists.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] The resource-timing buffer may truncate the cold-open counts. `requests_total`, `card_reads`, `format_check_queue_position` and the push image counts all read `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')`. That buffer holds 250 entries by default and silently drops the rest, and the largest recorded cold open is 216 requests. Nothing has shown it overflowing yet. [`scripts/cdp_harness.py:393-408`, `:784-789`] Options: (a) call `performance.setResourceTimingBufferSize` in the document-start observers; (b) cross-check `requests_total` against the companion's access log once first; (c) leave it. Recommended: (a) — one line, and it removes a silent ceiling under a release-gating measurement.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] `validate_data_layer.py` is a stale Epic 1 script. `create_test_dataset(Path("/tmp"))` fails on Windows wherever `\tmp` does not exist. The "query performance" check takes the max over every result, database initialization and the import included. `engine.dispose()` is skipped on error, and the `finally` then unlinks an open file (`PermissionError` on Windows). It still prints "Epic 1 … is COMPLETE!". Nothing references it, and it is the only caller of `find_by_colors`, `find_by_type` and `search_by_keywords`. [`scripts/validate_data_layer.py:198`, `:375`, `:389-394`, `:424`] Options: (a) delete it, together with B2's unused-repository-surface option (a); (b) fix it and keep it as a smoke script. Recommended: (a) — the unit and integration suites cover the data layer through real entry points, and deleting the script frees B2's three methods.

- [ ] [Review][Patch] `panels` destroys the database in any `--data-dir` it is given. It unlinks every `cards.db*` there and then writes `b"not a sqlite database"` into `cards.db`, with no `refuse_the_operators_data_dir` gate. `panels --data-dir "%LOCALAPPDATA%\artificial-planeswalker"` therefore deletes the card data and every saved deck. The module docstring still says `budget`, `push` and `panels` "only read a data directory". The default `mkdtemp` dir is never removed either. Gate it like `push --arm drain`, correct the docstring, and remove the temp dir it created [`scripts/cdp_harness.py:1923-1931`, `:1948`, `:32-34`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] Three subcommands call `companion.start()` before the `try` that stops the companion. If `await_up` raises after 120 s, the child keeps serving and holds the port and `cards.db`, which is trap 4 in the module's own docstring. `cmd_refetch` already moved `start()` inside its `try` ("trap 4, which this command already learned once"). The fix reached one of four sites [`scripts/cdp_harness.py:422`, `:979`, `:1934`; compare `:1740-1743`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `Browser.__init__` launches Chrome and creates the temp profile before steps that can raise: `_await_debugger`, `/json/list`, the `next(...)` over page targets (`StopIteration`), `wsc.connect`, and the three `send`s. Because no object is returned, nothing closes them. The same leak happens when `_open_measured_page` or `_open_draining_page` fail after launch, and `cmd_push` assigns its browser before the inner `try` that would close it [`scripts/cdp_harness.py:134-163`, `:814-828`, `:888-912`, `:997-1002`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `Companion.await_up` counts any HTTP answer on the port as "up". It never checks `self.proc.poll()` or the `/health` identity. A child that exits at once wastes the full 120 s and then reports "companion did not start". That happens, for example, when the copied data dir holds the operator's live `companion.json`, which the harness's own `robocopy` advice copies. With `--port` set to a live companion's port, the harness drives the operator's real companion instead. Fail fast when the child has exited, and check the answering instance's identity [`scripts/cdp_harness.py:295-303`, `:1706`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] `Companion.start` passes `CARDS_DATABASE_URL` through to the child, and `src/paths.py:77-78` lets that variable win over `PLANESWALKER_DATA_DIR`. Strip it from the child's environment [`scripts/cdp_harness.py:282-283`, `:1649-1679`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The release-gating cold-open verdict has no evidence floor. - [ ] [Review][Patch] `probe_harness.py` does not own its environment or working directory. No `cwd=` is passed, and pytest applies `testpaths` only from the rootdir, so a path-form invocation from a subdirectory collects only that subtree. - [ ] [Review][Patch] `vitest_probe_harness.py`'s run timeout does not end a hang on Windows. `npm` resolves to `npm.cmd`, and on timeout `subprocess.run` kills only that direct child, then calls `communicate()`, which waits for the pipes to close. The surviving node and vitest grandchildren still hold them, so the "a hang ends as a REFUSAL" guarantee fails in exactly the deadlocked-worker case it was written for. Kill the process tree on timeout [`scripts/vitest_probe_harness.py:437-460`]

**Rejected**

- `low` — `build_plugin.py --out .` deletes the root `.claude-plugin/` and `.mcp.json`: both are tracked, so `git checkout` restores them. The `--out` flag is a documented scratch option, and a guard adds a branch for a self-inflicted slip.
- `low` — `scripts/view_deck.py` resolves a name by eager-loading every deck: it is a one-shot CLI over a handful of local decks, beside the AD-15-frozen viewer. Deleting it is B7's call.
- `low` — `Browser.close()` deletes the profile while Chrome helpers may still hold it: the only cost is a leftover `cdp-harness-*` dir in `%TEMP%`, and a retry loop is new code.
- `low` — on POSIX, `Companion.stop()` terminates only the `uv` launcher: uv forwards SIGTERM to its child on Unix, and the harness is run on Windows, where `taskkill /T` kills the tree.
- `low` — `taskkill /F` skips the lifespan's `remove_discovery`, leaving a stale `companion.json`: the next start probes it (about 2 s) and reclaims it. A graceful stop needs a signal path Windows does not have.
- `low` — splitting `cdp_harness.py` into a package to remove its near-copies: restructuring 2,000 lines costs more than the copies do. The copies' missing error handling is the patch above.
- `low` — `Companion.__init__` creates a missing `--data-dir`: the next step fails visibly on the missing `cards.db` or token.
- `low` — `_outstanding_card_images` has no ceiling, so network events from an active drain could delay the push: unshown. If it happened, the positive control would fail the run visibly, not score it.
- `low` — `_largest_deck(None)` fetches every deck's detail, and `_card_the_deck_does_not_hold` reads 20,000 rows: this is a developer tool run against a copy holding a few decks.

### 2026-11-08 — F1 UI API and state, part 1

**Code review complete.** 1 `decision-needed`, 8 `patch`, 0 `defer`, 23 rejected.

- **Group:** F1. **Reviewed revision:** `ee0263d652a46f021515860bbc2a19e3dac60eed` (no change under `ui/src/api/` or `ui/src/state/` up to `7a9dd9c`).
- **Files (3,988 lines; over the ~3,000 guideline, accepted rather than split, as the F2 note offered):** `ui/src/api/client.ts` (556), `schema.ts` (455); `ui/src/state/connection.ts` (192), `deckGroups.ts` (374), `faces.ts` (161), `formatCheck.ts` (229), `formatCheck.fixtures.ts` (277), `identity.ts` (67), `inspection.ts` (393), `panel.ts` (69), `poller.ts` (339), `socket.ts` (576), `systemState.ts` (300). Not reviewed: the generated `types.d.ts` and `openapi.json`, and F2's `agentView.ts`, `deck.ts` and `cards.ts`. The colocated suites were read as evidence only; T1 covers them.
- **Mode:** no-code, thorough; lenses blind-hunter, edge-case-hunter, verification-gap, intent-alignment (none failed). Triage: leave as action items.
- **Overlap:**
  - The faces patch repeats F2's `cards.ts` inherited-key patch in another store. Fix both together, and grep every id-indexed plain object in `ui/src`.
  - The `deckGroups.ts` patch touches research F1 (one land and type definition). Merge in step 4.
  - The rejected `readFormatCheck` abort is the 2026-09-03 audit's open P3 "UI abort signals". Merge it with F2's rejected boot-reads abort.
  - The decision below affects F2's three files too: `agentView.ts` and `cards.ts` cite the same deleted suites.
- **What holds:**
  - **Dedupe and cancellation:**
    - Every loop (poller, socket, identity, format check) guards each await with a generation counter, and `stop()` bumps the counter as well, so a stale answer never writes and never forks a second timer chain.
    - The socket bumps the generation on every failure, so a browser's `error` + `close` pair costs one timer.
    - Emits fire only on change, and `applyInstanceId` is change-detected.
    - `request` is the one `fetch`, with a 10 s timeout (twice the SQLite busy timeout), and its manual `AbortSignal.any` fallback leaks nothing (the caller signal is per refetch, `deck.ts:378`).
    - The only request-level dedupe is `cards.ts`'s in-flight map (F2).
  - **Error states:**
    - Every reader is total and maps a missing response to `unreachable`, distinct from a decided refusal.
    - `panelFor` and `RETRIES_QUIETLY` are read, not copied.
    - The stalled escalation needs both elapsed time and observed refusals.
    - A restarted poll always writes its first answer (CAP-3).
    - `'live'` re-drives the deck boot (CAP-2), and `deck_changed` restarts the poll only if it has stopped.
  - **Boundaries:** `client.ts` validates every body before it reaches a store. The socket dispatch over the six event kinds fails to compile on a seventh. `connection.ts` composes the three slices without a cycle.

- [ ] [Review][Decision] The source comments cite UI gate suites that no longer exist, and nothing else enforces the rules they describe. Commit `e584980` (2026-09-04, audit story 2) deleted 26 source-reading `ui/tests` files, among them `store-writes.test.ts`, `posture.test.ts`, `copy-tails.test.ts` and `wire-contract.test.ts`. About twenty comments still name them as the enforcement, and some justify a module's shape by them:
  - `connection.ts` is said to exist so that `deck.ts` never names `useSystemStore`, because `store-writes.test.ts:112-113` would flag it [`ui/src/state/connection.ts:15`].
  - `faces.ts` has no `readFaceIndex` because "the scan" would see it [`ui/src/state/faces.ts:26`, `:81`, `:161`].
  - `client.ts` is "the one door", and `posture.test.ts` is said to assert it [`ui/src/api/client.ts:3`, `:30`, `:223`].
  - `keepsRetrying` exists so that `copy-tails`' fourth tail is checkable [`ui/src/state/socket.ts:264`, `:384`].
  - The rest are in `systemState.ts:47`, `:105`, `:123`, `:162`, `:187`; `inspection.ts:16`, `:76`, `:138`; `formatCheck.ts:20`; `identity.ts:8`; and `schema.ts:17`.

  `eslint.config.js` has no `no-restricted-globals` or `no-restricted-imports` rule for `fetch`, `WebSocket` or `localStorage`. So "one network door", "one writer per store" and "no persisted UI state" are now unenforced conventions that the comments say are gated. An editor who trusts a comment expects a gate to fire, and none will. Options:
  - (a) Reword every citation to state the rule as a convention, and name what enforces it today, if anything.
  - (b) Restore the cheap halves as ESLint rules, then do (a) for the rest. The cheap halves are `fetch` and `WebSocket` restricted outside `src/api/client.ts`, and `localStorage` and `document.cookie` banned in `src/state/`. These are linter rules, not source-scanning tests, so they stay within the 2026-09-04 "lint-as-tests out" ruling.
  - (c) Delete the citations without replacing them.

  Recommended: (b). The network door and the no-persistence rule are architecture invariants (AD-4, UX-DR15) that a one-line lint rule holds. The one-writer-per-store rule has no cheap lint form, so it becomes stated convention.
- [ ] [Review][Patch] **The socket resets its backoff on every open, so an accept-then-drop backend reconnects every 2 s forever.** `succeed()` zeroes `failures`, `failingSince` and `delay` as soon as `onopen` fires.
  - **Trigger:** a backend that accepts the upgrade and then closes it. `ws.py:520` closes `1011` on any post-accept fault, and `ws.py:402` closes on a failed broadcast send.
  - **Effect:** the loop never reaches `'down'`, so the Disconnected panel never shows, and the pill flickers between live and reconnecting.
  - **Cost per cycle:** a `'live'` (`refreshInstanceId` and a full `reconcileDeckBoot`, which is the active-deck, deck and format-check reads) plus `onReconnected` (`restartPoll` and `resetCardAttempts`). That is about six requests every 2 s.
  - **Fix:** reset the failure count and the backoff only once a socket has stayed open for a minimum period. Emitting `'live'` on open can stay as it is.
  - **Test:** in `socket.test.ts`, an open → immediate close loop that reaches `'down'` and backs off [`ui/src/state/socket.ts:436-446`].
- [ ] [Review][Patch] **The face store reads inherited object keys as face indexes.** `faces` is a plain object indexed by card id, and agent-view ids come off the wire with no shape (AD-7, F2's `cards.ts` patch).
  - **`useFaceIndex('constructor')`** returns `Object` rather than `0`, so the tile's face URL and the control's `aria-pressed` read a function.
  - **`flipCard`** computes `(Object + 1) % 2`, which is `NaN`: exactly the `?face=NaN` its docstring says it prevents.
  - **Fix:** read through `Object.hasOwn`, or use a null-prototype record. Fix it together with F2's `cards.ts` patch.
  - **Test:** add a `"constructor"` id to `faces.test.ts` [`ui/src/state/faces.ts:132`, `:151`].
- [ ] [Review][Patch] **`deckGroups.ts`'s header rests on a payload that no longer exists.** It says the deck row carries a bounded `CardSummary` "and no `card_faces`", so a `'Card // Card'` printing is "latent… rather than closed by fetching 99 card records".
  - **What changed:** `schema.ts:66-78` records that `DeckCardFull` now embeds the whole `Card`, so `card_faces[0].type_line` is already in the payload. The real front-face type is free, and those printings (2,274 in the corpus) still group as `Other`.
  - **`schema.ts`'s `CardFace` docstring** has the same stale premise: "hydration fills in place for 6% of a deck". `seedDeckCards` hydrates every row.
  - **Fix:** correct both docstrings, and resolve the front-face type from `card_faces[0]` when the top-level line is the degenerate `Card // Card`. Merge this with research F1's single type rule in step 4.
  - **Test:** a `deckGroups.test.ts` case for a `'Card // Card'` row [`ui/src/state/deckGroups.ts:15-22`, `ui/src/api/schema.ts:127-132`].
- [ ] [Review][Patch] **Two guarded paths have no test that would fail if the guard were removed.**
  - **The socket's `finally`:** `fail()` puts `schedule(next)` in `finally` so that a throwing `onStatus` cannot stop the loop. Every `onStatus` in `socket.test.ts` only records, so simplifying `fail()` to `emit(); schedule()` passes the whole suite.
    - Add the throwing-`onStatus` case beside the throwing-constructor test (`socket.test.ts:871`).
    - It must prove a second mint at `SOCKET_BASE_MS`.
  - **`unreachable` resets the stalled clock:** the escalation guard runs before the `unreachable` early return, so a network rejection resets the clock, as the docstring states. No test feeds an `unreachable` into an active `database_unavailable` run, so moving the early return up passes.
    - Add `script(UNAVAILABLE ×4, UNREACHABLE, UNAVAILABLE…)` to the "stalled escalation fires on ONE token only" block (`poller.test.ts:247`).
    - Assert no stall at 60 s from the first refusal, and a stall 60 s after the post-blip refusals.

  [`ui/src/state/socket.ts:429-433`, `ui/src/state/poller.ts:237-246`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] **A second click cannot release a pin whose card became the unknown-card placeholder.** `togglePin` refuses when the card is not inspectable *before* it checks for a release.
  - **Trigger:** an agent-view thumbnail pinned while unseen (inspectable), which then hydrates to `unknown-card`. Clicking it again does nothing; only Esc or the panel's unpin control release it.
  - **Fix:** apply the refusal only to pinning, never to releasing the card already pinned.

Renumber the list, with one entry covering all four push kinds [`ui/src/state/connection.ts:21-67`].
- [ ] [Review][Patch] **Comments hard-code counts and line numbers that have drifted.**
  - **Field and store counts:**

    - `inspection.ts` says "names all four" stores, `faces.ts` says "all five", and `formatCheck.ts` calls itself "a sixth store"; `ui/src` has seven.

    - `socket.ts` cites `poller.ts:161-168` and `poller.ts:200-204`; the code is now at `:166-172` and `:214`.
