### 2026-11-09 — T1-2 tests: MCP tools and scripts (B7-B9, B13)

**Code review complete.** 1 `decision-needed`, 9 `patch`, 0 `defer`, 0 rejected.

- **Area:** T1-2, test design only. **Revision:** `ee74ae3807d41f59a061aa75af1b33a8d22ef5ef`. No file under `tests/` has changed since the reviewed revision `ee0263d`.
- **Mode:** no-code, quick. One context-free reviewer, which did not fail. Triage: leave as action items.
- **Files (5 files, 3,103 lines).** Each was picked for the B7-B9/B13 risk it covers:
  - `tests/integration/test_mcp_tools.py` (2,335): T1-0's slow cluster (29 of the 30 slowest tests), the in-process client harness, the `assess` / `compare` paths, B7's setup-tool round trips and the tool-list guard.
  - `tests/integration/conftest.py` (265) and `tests/conftest.py` (25): the shared fixtures (`seeded_card_db`, `seeded_vec_db`) and the autouse companion-client reset that the slow file uses.
  - `tests/integration/mcp_server/test_first_run_data_init.py` (451): B7's setup tools (the `prune=true` pin, the `rebuild` gap) and the `database_not_initialized` guard matrix. `test_mcp_tools.py` imports its `_fake_importer` and `_sync_cards_factory`.
  - `tests/test_setup.py` (27): in scope and small.
- **Left out on purpose:**
  - `tests/integration/test_build_plugin.py` (730): B13 already recorded its two items (the import-time `logging.basicConfig` and the snake_case docstring), and `_assert_bundles_match` was cleared by T1-0.
  - The other `tests/integration/mcp_server/` suites (about 8,000 lines): the B8 and B9 verification-gap lenses read them and recorded their gaps. The reviewer read `test_assess_deck_power_tool.py`, `test_compare_deck_power_tool.py`, `test_deck_changed_wiring.py` and `test_deck_management_tool.py` as evidence only, for the duplication and coverage comparisons below.
- **Not repeated:** the test patches the B7-B9/B13 blocks already record (B7's `rebuild=True` and `update_deck` strip-emit tests; B8's session-release, quantity-branch and write-arm tests; B9's rollback, gap-direction, lookup, search, seed and forwarding tests; B13's harness and migration tests), and T1-1's shared `in_memory_engine` / `test_db` patch. A finding below that extends one of them says what is new.
- **Why `test_mcp_tools.py` is slow (T1-0's open question, now verified):** the time is the companion notifier, not the MCP client. Each mutating call's `deck_changed` emit reads the developer's real discovery file. On this machine that file is stale (`companion.json`, written 2026-09-15, port 8765), so each emit waits on a dead port until the 1.0 s `_NOTIFY_TOTAL_SECONDS` budget cuts it off. The mutation counts match T1-0's steps: 11 calls for the two 11.44 s compare tests, 8 for the 8.34 s assess test, 2 for each 2.16 s `set_card_quantity` case. That is about 135 s of the top 30's ~146 s. The non-mutating client tests, and the two that stub `_notify_deck_changed`, never reach the list. CI's Linux runners have no discovery file, so T1-0's ranking describes one developer's machine.
- **What holds:**
  - The client harness is a real entry point: `build_server` plus `create_connected_server_and_client_session`, in process, with no subprocess.
  - The `update_deck` / `set_card_quantity` / `clone_deck` block (`:320-776`) holds the only checks for the wire-only refusals (an unknown `changes` key, an omitted `quantity`). `test_deck_changed_wiring.py` drives the same tools for their emits.
  - The `database_not_initialized` matrix in `test_first_run_data_init.py` covers the semantic two-state contract.
  - The tests carry no assertion-free functions. T1-0's four survivors are all outside this scope.

- [ ] [Review][Decision] The assessment client block mostly repeats the helper suites. Shape parity, byte determinism, sorted lists, delta-equals-subtraction and the four degradation paths are each pinned in `test_assess_deck_power_tool.py` (`:990`, `:1070`, `:1100`, `:668`, `:790`, `:811`, `:829`) or `test_compare_deck_power_tool.py` (`:379`, `:738`). The client copies add three things: the wire text equals `structuredContent`; `confidence.reasons` / `level` are compared as structures, where the helper twins check substrings of `summary`; and the bracket and combo-bucket flips run after a real `add_card_to_deck`. Once the data-dir patch below lands, each test costs about 0.2 s, so this is about duplication, not time. [`tests/integration/test_mcp_tools.py:984-2016`] Options: (a) keep both layers as they are; (b) move the structured `confidence` assertions into the helper twins, and keep one wire test per tool plus the edit-driven flips; (c) delete the client copies without moving anything. Recommended: (a) — after the fix the cost is small, and the wire and edit-driven checks have no helper equivalent; revisit (b) if the assessment contract changes.
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The MCP client tests run against the developer's real data directory. Only `closed_companion` (`:2137`, two tests) pins `PLANESWALKER_DATA_DIR`. Every other mutation reaches the real `notify_deck_changed`, which reads `%LOCALAPPDATA%\artificial-planeswalker\companion.json` (`client.py:337`). With a stale file, that costs 1 s per mutation (about 135 s locally; see the note above). With a live companion, the suite sends it authenticated `deck_changed` POSTs carrying test deck ids, the hazard `test_deck_changed_wiring.py:1003-1018` documents. `build_server`'s default `ConnectionFactory()` also resolves the real data directory (`server.py:185-186`). Add an autouse fixture in `tests/conftest.py`, beside `_fresh_shared_companion_client`, that points `PLANESWALKER_DATA_DIR` at an empty temp dir, and remove the per-file copies (`test_entry_point.py:113`, `test_deck_changed_wiring.py:899`, `:1013`, `test_companion_tool.py:1232`, `test_companion_degradation.py:67`, `closed_companion`). Tests of the default `paths` location then `delenv`. Re-run `--durations=30` afterwards to confirm the 1 s steps are gone [`tests/integration/test_mcp_tools.py:2137`, `tests/conftest.py:14`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The assessment builders and fixtures are copied three times, against G1's "consolidate before the second copy" rule (`tests/fixtures/assessment.py:3-5`). `_assessment_card` (whose docstring says it "mirrors" the other) has the same body as `_card` in the assess and compare suites. The Krenko / Zada / Game Changer seeds are near-copies. `_make_deck` and `uninitialized_session` are byte-identical across the assess and compare suites. `_RecordingNotifier` is copied in `test_deck_changed_wiring.py:162`. `test_mcp_tools.py` imports private helpers from a test module (`_fake_importer`, `_sync_cards_factory`). Move them into `tests/fixtures/`. This is new against T1-1's patch, which covers `in_memory_engine` / `session` / `test_db` only [`tests/integration/test_mcp_tools.py:62-65`, `:345`, `:999`; `tests/integration/mcp_server/test_assess_deck_power_tool.py:65`, `:198`, `:208`; `test_compare_deck_power_tool.py:249`, `:343`, `:353`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] The search-index client tests point the server's two database handles at different files. The session factory opens `seeded_card_db`'s `test.db` (3 cards); `_sync_cards_factory` opens `cards.db` (2 cards, so `cards_indexed == 2`). Production resolves both to one file (`server.py:170-174`). So the concurrency test's `list_decks` never reads the file the build is writing: it proves the work runs off the loop, not that a read succeeds while the build holds the file. `_sync_cards_factory` also hand-types the `cards` DDL without `printed_name`, `color_identity`, `game_changer` and others, a third copy beside T1-1's `test_index_builder.py` / `test_query.py` item. Build one file through `init_database`, seed it, and give both seams that path, as `seeded_vec_db` does [`tests/integration/test_mcp_tools.py:2060`, `:2092`; `tests/integration/mcp_server/test_first_run_data_init.py:76-81`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] Nothing checks which bulk type `initialize_database` imports, or that the wrapper forwards `update`. Every fake importer accepts `bulk_type` (with a default of `"oracle_cards"`) and ignores it, so changing `_DEFAULT_BULK_TYPE` to `"oracle_cards"` passes the suite and brings back the single-printing `games` and the Arena false positives (the regression B13's migration-advice patch names). The client round trip sends only `{}`, so deleting `update=update` from the wrapper (`server.py:1192`) passes too; the `update=True` tests call the helper directly. Have the fake record `bulk_type` and assert `"default_cards"`, and add a `{"update": true}` client call that expects `updated`. This extends B7's `rebuild=True` patch: the same never-driven forward, on the other setup tool [`tests/integration/mcp_server/test_first_run_data_init.py:61`, `tests/integration/test_mcp_tools.py:2021-2038`, `src/mcp_server/tools/initialize_database.py:44`]
- [ ] [Review][Patch] Four helpers' `database_not_initialized` guards are tested nowhere: `load_deck`, `delete_deck`, `remove_card_from_deck` and `detect_synergies`. `test_first_run_data_init.py:10` claims "one per result model", but it has no `DeckDeleteResult` or `SynergyResult` case, and the wiring suite covers only `clone_deck`, `create_deck`, `update_deck` and `set_card_quantity`. On an empty card table these helpers would answer `deck_not_found` without the guard, so deleting any of the four passes. Parametrise one test over every guarded helper [`src/mcp_server/tools/deck_management.py:413`, `:564`, `:880`; `src/mcp_server/tools/deck_analysis.py:224`; `tests/integration/mcp_server/test_first_run_data_init.py:333-384`]

Drop the `sorted` and both calls emit the same order, so the byte comparison still passes; only the two-name list assertions catch it, on about half of runs. The helper twins (`test_assess_deck_power_tool.py:1070`, `:1100`) have the same gap.
